11. In GCMS Scroll down to the Client Details section. Review the Biometric Assessment status for every applicant on the application.
The Biometrics assessment types are as follows BEFORE PROMOTION:

Required: Client needs Biometrics – this is normal and what is needed Received – NRT: Hold the file and email RA Complete: Client has Biometrics – hold the file and email RA Exempt: Client does not need Biometrics Not Required: Client does not need Biometrics

NOC 1315 Age Date of Birth 1990-08-04 Changed No Change

In the above example, the Status Updated date for the ITA was March 29, 2023. The

# application Received Date was April 5, 2023. The client's birth date was August 4th.

Therefore there was no change in age, the birth date did not fall between the ITA date and

the date the file was received. If the birthdate was April 1st, there would be a change.

## If a client's DOB falls on the ITA, mark that the age has NOT changed.

If a client's DOB falls on the received date of the application, mark the age HAS changed.

16. In GCMS, click Notes tab. Verify any notes that have been added by CSU, the call center, CPC-O, Registry, any relevant file notes regarding police certificates, or if there is an application withdrawal request. If the applicant requests a withdrawal of their EE application, the Program Assistant will promote the application and see PNP Insert for withdrawal instructions.
17. Look for potential Statutory Questions. If the client answered “Yes" to any Statutory Question, the details should be provided within the Notes. If the client provides details for stat question the following generates in the EE Eligibility note from the ADMM when the right items are toggled: PA/SP: Statutory details located in separate GCMS note

18. Verify if PA has a Representative or Designated Individual included in the Notes. Appendix D outlines how to determine if a Rep is associated or not associated to an application. (If a Designated Individual is included, look for the IMM5475 in incoming correspondence, if not provided, park the file after promotion, and request the form.)
# If the REP is not associated to the application DO NOT associate a Party ID

to the application, this breaks the link with IRCC Secure Account.

# A rep does NOT have to be associated to be part of the application.

21. Once the History Pull is complete, go into GCMS, review the Stat Questions tab under Background Information. The pull should stop in the Background Info tab. Notes AdmissibilitiesAddresses PaymentsMedicals-HB Summary CasesDocuments Benefits

# Scroll down and verify that the client has answered all questions (Yes or No, depending

on what was provided in the notes. (match up the yes's to the notes you had reviewed, if applicable. Ex: Refused Refugee status was in notes – the yes should go with that line)

The client is able to enter notes (in the Note tab) to respond to Statutory Questions where they have answered “Yes” (this is not required if the client had entered Stat Questions in the Note Tab)

If the client answered yes to a question and did not provide the additional

## details (in the subtab – additional details, the Notes tab, or within incoming

correspondence), request a Schedule A form (after promotion) If the application only requires additional stat details; finish processing the application, send request for Schedule A, and move the file forward (you do not have to park an application for additional Statutory Questions details alone)

complete, request a completed and signed Additional Family Information form (IMM 5406) for the appropriate client and park the file for 7 days. Even if the information pulls in after promotion.

- When the IMM5406 is received, the program assistant will review the Additional Family Information form (IMM5406) and move the file forward if no concerns. If concerns are found (i.e. eligible family member (SP/CL/DEP child) is listed on IMM5406 form but not included on e-APR under Clients & Parties), the program assistant will park the file and send the RA an email requesting review of family members listed on IMM5406. File will remain parked until the RA provides direction on how to proceed.
- If the IMM5406 is not provided within 7 days, the program assistant will reject the file as R10 incomplete – requested documents not provided

A review of Address and Background Info is completed for all applicants on the file 18 years of age and older. Review information for the 10 years prior to the application received date OR since the applicant's 18th birthday whichever date is most recent.

Ex: Application is received on April 3, 2023. If the applicant's DOB is August 13, 1985 (age 37 at time of application), they should have provided complete address & personal histories since April 3, 2013; last 10 years.

If the applicant's DOB is August 13, 1999 (age 18 at time of application), they should have

## provided complete address & personal histories since their; since 18th birthday, August

13, 2017. The ADMM calculates their 18th birthdate for review automatically.

NOTE: If the correct time frames are NOT provided a Schedule A must be requested. This includes if the time at the beginning that is provided by the client is not included ex: not within the last ten years or since turning 18. Be mindful for this information, ensure the client history starts at the correct date.

### 49. POLICE CERTIFICATES (PC): Police Certificates

A foreign national (18years of age or older)applying for permanent residence must provide a PC. A PC from each country in which the client has resided/travelled to/studied in for

a period of 6 months or more in a row for the last 10 years, or since their 18th birthday (whichever comes first) is required For the client's current country of residence, the PC must be issued no more than six months before the submission of the application For countries in which the client no longer resides, the PC must have been issued after the last time the client lived in that country for 6 months or more in a row and cover the full period of time they resided there.

APPENDIX B NOC SKILL LEVEL

The NOC (National Occupation Classification) should be skill level 0, A, or B. To determine what skill level a client's NOC is, refer to the NOC Coding System below: NOC CODING SYSTEM Skill 0/Teer 0 - Any NOC that starts with a 0 Skill A/Teer 1 - When the second digit is a 0 or 1 Skill B/Teer 2 and Teer 3 - When the second digit is a 2 or 3

Skill type or level (NOC 2016) compared to TEER category (NOC 2021) Skill type or level (NOCTEER category

# 2016) (NOC 2021)

Skill type 0 TEER O Skill level A TEER 1 Skill level B TEER 2 and TEER 3

***The second digit of the new NOC 2021 is what determines what TEER category. If the 2nd digit is a 0 then its TEER 0. If the 2nd digit is a 1 then its TEER 1. If the second digit is a 2 then its TEER 2. If the second digit is a 3 then it's TEER 3 etc. Skill type or level (NOC 2016) compared to TEER category (NOC 2021) Skill type 0 = TEER 0 Skill level A = TEER 1 Skill level B = TEER 2 and TEER 3 Skill level C = TEER 4 Skill level D = TEER 5 For Express Entry, this means that occupations in TEERs 0, 1, 2, 3 are eligible to meet program requirements, previously structured as NOC 0, A, B.

### PNP NOC CODE Discrepancies between GCMS/Spreadsheets:

If there is a NOC Discrepancy between the NOC provided in GCMS and the NOC listed on the PN Certificate/Spreadsheet please enter the following separate note in GCMS: Discrepancy between the NOC added by the Client NOC **** and the NOC as per PN

# Certificate/Spreadsheet NOC ****

## ***Important 2021 NOC Code changes to remember for all LOBs***

When file indicates NOC 2021:

1. New Worker (Spouse/CLP NOC to be updated) 9914 is now 09914
2.Student (Children 5 and over) 9911 is now 09911
3. Dependent (Children under 5) 9980 is now 09980
4. Specialist physicians – NOC 3111 (NOC 2016) is now Specialists in clinical and laboratory medicine – NOC 31100 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for All LOB)
5. Specialist physicians – NOC 3111 (NOC 2016) is now Specialists in surgery – NOC 31101 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for All LOB)
6. General practitioners and family physicians – NOC 3112 (NOC 2016), is now General practitioners and family physicians – NOC 31102 (NOC 2021) (Require MCC for ECA for AlI LOB)
7. Pharmacists-NOC 3131 (NOC 2016), is now Pharmacists – NOC 31120 (NOC 2021) *** If you determine your file is a rejection based on language test / benchmarks etc DO NOT REJECT – hold the file and send to the RA for review***

If Rep associated to the application is different than the Rep listed in the Notes or if the Rep listed in Notes Tab is different than the Rep found on the IMM5476:

- Clarification is required if the client does not upload the Use of Representative form (IMM5476) appointing one Rep and cancelling another
- Park the file for 7 days and request a new IMM5476 using the following:
It appears you have two representatives associated to your application for permanent residence. As noted on the top of the IMM5476, you may have only one representative per application. Please provide a newly completed and signed Use of Representative (IMM5476) form appointing one representative. Also, ensure you complete Section C: Cancel the Appointment of a Representative listing the individual you do not request to represent you.

## When the new IMM5476 is received:

- We will NOT associate the new rep under the Clients & Parties tab, this will break the link with IRCC secure account for the client.
- If the PA is cancelling the Rep listed under Clients & Parties tab, we will disassociate this rep from the application If the PA is appointing the Rep listed under application notes, we will NOT associate the new rep under Clients & Parties, this will break the IRCC secure account link. For this case, add a new General note:
Rep _was cancelled and new Rep__was appointed as per IMM5476 rec'd (See edoc_)

If a Release of Information (IMM5475) is not submitted:

- Park the file for 7 days and request an IMM5475. Under outgoing correspondence send the client a request letter for the Release of Information IMM5475 It is not necessary to associate the Designated Individual to the application, review the form upon arrival to confirm it is the same Designated Individual provided in the Notes tab
- If after a request letter is sent and the form is not provided within the time frame provided to the client, do not reject. Proceed with the file, do not note the designated individual in the EE Eligibility note.

MANDATORY DOCUMENTS Information obtained December 19th 2017. Make note the PDI is updated regularly. For the most up-to-date information on mandatory documents; use the web link listed below.

## Translation of Documents

For PCs: If a certificate is in a language other than English or French, the client must provide IRCC with a copy of the original document as well as a translation completed by a certified translator.

For all other Documents: If a supporting document is in a language other than English or French, the applicant must provide IRCC with:

- the English or French translation; and
- an affidavit from the person who completed the translations; and
- a certified photocopy of the original document
The instruction guide for Express Entry candidates and applicants explains how to meet these requirements.

## MANDATORY DOCUMENTS FOR ALL APPLICANTS

The following documents are mandatory and must be provided by all applicants.

Applications that do not contain the following documents will be rejected as incomplete, and fees will be refunded accordingly.

## Medical Examination Confirmation

Program Assistant Tip: It is acceptable if a copy of the medical has not been uploaded in the applicant's correspondence but is viewable within the client's T-number.

Purpose: To assess the health of the applicant and their family members

## Document requirements

•The medical examination confirmation is required up front and must be valid at the time of application. •The applicant must include the immigration medical examination (IME) number in their e-APR. The applicant must provide a copy of either the information printout sheet or the Upfront Medical Report form [IMM 1017B, whichever the panel physician provides upon completion of the medical examination. Exceptions are made only in rare circumstances that are outside of the client's control (e.g., cancellation of the appointment by the panel physician at the last minute without time to reschedule).

## In exceptional circumstances, IRCC may accept

- proof that a medical appointment is scheduled; or
- a letter of explanation of best efforts.
However, these are not a guarantee of acceptance.

## Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner
- Their dependent children, including non-accompanying
# 97 I Page

2A-2024-71640-000067

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

s.16(1)(b)
## Police Certificates

Purpose: To determine if the applicant or their family members have a criminal record or poses a security risk to Canada

Document requirements

According to the requirements below, 1

- For the applicant's current country of residence, the police certificate must have been issued no more than six months before the submission of the e-APR. For countries in which the applicant no longer resides, the police certificate must have been issued after the last time the applicant stayed in that country for 6 months or more in a row.
- Police certificates meeting the above requirements can be accepted even if they have an expiry date that has passed. This includes the requirement to provide a police certificate for countries in which the individual has travelled for 6 months or more in a row, whether or not the individual had an established residential address during this time.
- Police certificates are required upfront and are mandatory for each country (except Canada) where an individual has spent 6 months or more in a row within the last 10 years. The individual does not need to provide one for any period of time before the age of 18.
- Police certificates need to be a scan of the original police certificate(s) in colour. Certified true copies and unauthorized copies are unacceptable and will result in the application being rejected as incomplete.
Applications that do not include a required police certificate and do not provide supporting documentation where required will be rejected as incomplete.

The officer can exercise discretion when assessing whether police certificates that do not fall within standard IRCC parameters may still be required to process the application. When necessary, an officer can use discretion in their determination of whether or not a client is admissible to Canada by using other documents or mechanisms that are available to them.

Some countries will not issue police certificates to applicants, and instead will only communicate directly with the relevant Canadian authorities. In such cases, existing IRCC procedures to obtain documentation should be followed.

The IRCC website provides instructions to applicants on Where to obtain a police certificate. In exceptional circumstances, IRCC may accept both of the following:

- proof of having requested a police certificate for such countries; and
- An explanation of best efforts (not a guarantee of acceptance). In this explanation, the applicant should explain the delay in a document and
upload the document in the country-specific field. The uploaded document must show that the applicant requested a police certificate as soon as possible after receiving their invitation to apply. In such exceptional cases, the client will be advised to upload a document into the upload field stating country-specific details that support their explanation of best efforts. Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner
- Their dependent children 18 years of age or older, including non-accompanying
Copy of the Biographical data page of a Passport or Travel Document Purpose: To confirm the applicant's identity Document requirements

- A clear and legible copy of the biographical data page of a passport or travel document must be provided
Program Assistant Tip: (If available) ensure the MRZ lines of the PPT bio data page are visible. The majority of the client's information will be verified using the MRZ lines.

Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner
- Their accompanying dependent children
Proof of Work Experience Purpose: To prove each work experience claim and to validate that the applicant meets program requirements

Document requirements

- The personalized document checklist in applicants' online account prompts the applicant with an upload field for each work experience declared. At the completeness check, the processing office determines only whether the required documents are included. The validity or fraudulence of the information is assessed only if and when the application is placed into processing.

The following documents are mandatory for each work experience declared:

- A reference or experience letter from the employer, which should be an official document printed on company letterhead (must include the applicant's name, the company's contact information [address, telephone number and email address], and the name, title and signature of the immediate supervisor or personnel officer at the company), should indicate all positions held while employed at the company and must include the following details: job title, duties and responsibilities, job status (if current job), dates worked for the company, number of work hours per week and annual salary plus benefits; and
- If the applicant is self-employed, articles of incorporation or other evidence of business ownership, evidence of self-employment income and documentation from third-party individuals indicating the service provided along with payment details (self-declared main duties or affidavits are not acceptable proof of self-employed work experience).

If the work experience is in Canada, proof may include copies of T4 tax information slips and notices of assessment issued by the Canada Revenue Agency (the time period for these documents should reflect the work experience timeframe [e.g., work experience from 2006 to 2008 requires only documents from those calendar years]).

## Individuals who must submit this documentation

- The principal applicant
- Their spouse or common-law partner (if work experience in Canada is claimed)

# Mandatory Documents (if applicable)

The following documents are mandatory if applicable (i.e., based on the personal circumstances of the applicant and their dependents). When applicable, applications that do not contain the following documents will be rejected as incomplete and fees will be refunded.

Copy of Birth Certificate or Other Equivalent Document Program Assistant Tip: Ensure the name of at least one parent is included on the child's birth certificate. Birth Certificates based on Passports are not acceptable.

Purpose: To prove the dependent children's relationship to the parents Document requirements

- This document is required only if the applicant identifies dependent children.
- The document must be one of the following: birth certificate; equivalent document from the birth country; or letter of explanation (where no birth certificate is available in the country).
Individuals who must submit this documentation

- All the dependent children, including non-accompanying
Proof of Studies Program Assistant Tip: ONLY mandatory for FSW or PNP-FSW LOB for the PA only

Purpose: To confirm education claims and validate that the applicant meets program requirements Document requirements

- To receive program-specific points for education, the applicant must either provide proof of having completed a Canadian secondary or post-secondary
educational program; or obtain an Educational Credential Assessment (ECA) and provide the ECA reference number in their application, along with proof of the foreign educational credentials for which points are being claimed.

- Proof of completed Canadian or foreign educational credentials can include copies of secondary or post-secondary education documents (certificates, diplomas or
degrees); and 0transcripts for successfully completed secondary or post-secondary studies, if available.

- Applicants must provide the Designated Learning Institution (DLI) number of the Canadian Institution where they completed their highest degree to be assigned points for their Canadian educational credentials. At the EE profile and application stage, the system will ask the candidate to enter
the # of their DLI by using the list of DLI posted on IRCC website. The system will insert a note if the number of the DLI doesn't match for the officer to review

- In cases where an applicant's background information suggests that a Canadian education credential may have been obtained via distance learning either abroad or while present in Canada, it is at the officer's discretion to request for supporting documentation. If it is determined that the applicant obtained more than 50 percent of their
education via distance learning, the applicant should not be awarded additional Comprehensive Ranking System (CRS) points for having a Canadian education credential. If the reduction in CRS points results in the applicant having a score below the minimum CRS, the application should be refused per A11.2.

- Where an ECA has been obtained, the applicant must provide the ECA reference number in their e-APR for online validation directly from the issuing organization. A scanned copy of the ECA is not required when submitting the e-APR but may
be requested by IRCC at a later date. Applicants are instructed to indicate that their education was completed by correspondence by typing in brackets {by correspondence} on the same line as the education institution name found in the Education Background Information tab of GCMS. ## the host country where it took place, not the country of nationality of the

### embassy or consulate.

- If the applicant married in Canada, a copy of the marriage certificate issued by the province or territory where the marriage took place must be provided.

## Other documents

As applicable to their situation, applicants are required to submit the following documents through their online account as part of their e-APR. The system does not let applicants submit their e-APR until documents have been uploaded into the provided fields.

In contrast to other documents, however, applications will not be rejected as “incomplete" if the documents listed below are not provided to the proper specification.

Applications can, however, be refused if the processing office does not have enough information on file to be satisfied that the applicant meets the requirements of the program to which they are applying.

Proof of relationship to a relative in Canada, status of a relative in Canada and residence status of a relative in Canada (including a brother or sister for the purpose of obtaining additional CRS points)

Purpose: To validate a claim of, and award points associated with, having

- a relative in Canada who is a Canadian citizen or permanent resident (for the Federal Skilled Worker Program adaptability points);
- an adult brother or sister who is a Canadian citizen or permanent resident residing in Canada (for additional CRS points).

Document requirements

•This documentation is required if the applicant indicates that they, or their accompanying spouse or common-law partner, have a family member in Canada who is a permanent resident or a Canadian citizen. •A copy of both sides of the family member's Canadian citizenship document, Canadian birth certificate and/or permanent resident card must be provided. Evidence that the family member currently resides in Canada must be provided (a residential lease, mortgage documents, utility bills, etc.). The documents must show their address in Canada and should be recent (dated within six months prior to submission). Proof of the relationship of the applicant or the accompanying spouse or common-law partner to the family member must also be provided (e.g., a birth certificate, an official document naming the applicant as a relative, a copy of the inside back cover of the relative's passport showing the relative's parents'marriage certificate, legal adoption documents and any other documents that prove or describe the relationship).

## Document requirements

- This document is not requested by the system, even if the applicant has indicated they are designating an individual.

## Document requirements

- Submission of this documentation is at the applicant's discretion. One upload field is provided for each of the applicant, their spouse or common-law partner and their dependent children, as applicable.
- Multiple documents must be compiled and uploaded as a single file. This should be used to provide documentation to support a declaration of status as “legally separated” (e.g., a letter of explanation, legal documents, etc.), if applicable.
- This should be used to submit the Authority to Release Personal Information to a Designated Individual form [IMM 5475 (PDF. 593.57 KB)], if applicable.

# Effective Immediately – Changes to ECA instructions for Express Entry

The guidelines below will be applied to all Express Entry lines of business – FSW, FST, CEC and PNP. Guidelines apply to both the PA & the SP. In other words, if a PA or SP have an ECA that cannot be verified on the application, the application will be rejected. Please see SITUATIONs section below and steps to take:

Note: When Reviewing Education on an Application

** Along with reviewing Incoming Correspondence, please review the Received lines for Education under EE Eligibility tab > Education sub tab.

SITUATIONS:

1.If the ECA assessment is pending or on hold as of the received date, then the application will be held for all Express Entry lines of business – FSW, FST, CEC and PNP. Refer to the TL for review via email. Template is located in Appendix GG.
2. If the ECA is expired as of the received date-rejection
3. (a)ECA (WES only)– ECAs from WES must indicate for immigration purposes.
If WES is not for immigration purposes – Reject. If the WES document cannot be verified-Request a copy if one was not provided. If the WES document is for immigration purposes and the copy cannot be verified-Send an email to the RA Inbox.

(b)All other ECAs– If you are able to verify – ok to continue. -If the ECA cannot be verified-Request a copy if one was not provided. If provided copy cannot be verified = Refer to the RA.
4. If the client has provided more than one education document type (i.e. Canadian Education and an ECA), but you are not able to verify one (or more) of the documents, refer to the ECA and Canadian Education Guidelines in this Appendix to determine if it must go to the RA or can be processed.
5. CANADIAN HIGH SCHOOL (SECONDARY EDUCATION) Although a Canadian High School diploma may be indicated and/or provided on an Express Entry application, it is not required and the absence of this document from the application is NOT considered a reason for rejection. Exception: If a Canadian high

1.Both Canadian credential provided and verifiable AND ECA provided but is not verifiable:
a. If Canadian Education is highest = note Canadian Education and add the following note for the ECA "unable to verify ECA."Note foreign credentials are provided or not, move forward.
b. If ECA the highest and unable to verify (not for immigration purposes, expired) = rejection
C. If ECA on hold or in progress-place the file on hold and email TL (Template found in Appendix GG.) Hold till advised to proceed.
d. If unable to determine which is the highest level of education = send to the RA with subject line: Highest Level of Education
2. Both ECA provided and verifiable AND Canadian credential provided under incoming correspondence:
a.If Canadian Education is verifiable (see #4 a or b.) = note and move forward
b. If Canadian Education is not verifiable (see #4 c, d, or e) = send to RA
3.Only Foreign education with ECA
a.If ECA verifiable = note and move forward
b.If ECA not able to verify (not for immigration purposes, expired) = reject
C. If ECA provided is for immigration purposes but you are unable to verify = send verification email as per R10 manual
d. If ECA on hold or in progress-place the file on hold and email TL (Template found in Appendix GG.) Hold till advised to proceed.
4. Only Canadian Education indicated under EE Eligibility received line:
a. If transcripts and diploma/certificate are provided = note and move forward
b.If only diploma/certificate provided = note and move forward
C.If only transcripts provided = send to RA
### d. If letter from university and transcripts provided = send to RA

e. If letter from University provided and no transcripts = send to RA fIf FSW or PNP-FSW and no education is provided (for PA only) = rejection
5.If no ECA is indicated under EE Eligibility received line = ECA not required, note if foreign credentials are provided or not and move forward
a.Exception for PA only: applying under FSW or PNP (FSW) and does not have a Canadian credential = rejection
6.1If no ECA is indicated under EE Eligibility received line but client provides ECA under incoming correspondence
a. If ECA verifiable = note and move forward
b. If ECA not verifiable (not for immigration purposes, on hold, in progress, expired) = enter "Foreign Credentials Provided" or "Foreign Credentials Not Provided" in note and move forward.
C. Exception for PA only: applying under FSW or PNP (FSW) and does not have a Canadian credential = rejection
7. If the PA's NOC code is either 31100, 31101 or 31102 the ECA MUST be issued by MCC for all LOB
a. ECA issued by MCC = note and move forward
b. ECA issued by any other provider and no MCC = rejection
C. MCC provided and verifiable and ECA provided but not verifiable = note and move forward
d. No MCC ECA # provided (ECA # field is blank and foreign credential provided) = send to RA

## HOW TO VERIFY CANADIAN EDUCATION

The client may provide proof of completed Canadian Education (ex. transcripts, a copy of a diploma, certificate, a letter from the university, etc.) If only a letter or transcript is provided with no accompanying diploma or certificate please refer to the RA for review.

Verify that the Institution's legal status is Registered, Authorized, or Recognized using the online verification site.

[https://www.cicic.ca/868/Search-the-Directory-of-Educational-Institutions-in-Canada/index.canada](https://www.cicic.ca/868/Search-the-Directory-of-Educational-Institutions-in-Canada/index.canada) .ca INDIVIDUAL Find an educational institution

## HOW TO NOTE CANADIAN EDUCATION

- ALL CANADIAN EDUCATION MUST BE NOTED, EVEN IF THERE'S A HIGHER LEVEL PROVIDED (ex: Certificate AND Bachelor's degree, both are noted) If the client provided proof of a completed Canadian degree, (Bachelors, Masters, Doctorate) the education should be listed as “Bachelor's Degree, Master's Degree, Doctorate" (whichever is applicable) in your EE Eligibility note
- If the client provided proof of a completed Canadian certificate, diploma, or associate degree the Program Assistant will attempt to determine the length of the program and include this information in the EE Eligibility note. Examples of note: Certificate (1 year) or 2 Year Diploma. Occasionally the certificate or diploma provided by the client will indicate the
length of the program on it oOther times the client will provide transcripts or a letter from the institution to accompany the certificate or diploma and they may be used to determine the length of the program If the length of the program is unclear, the Program Assistant should note Education as Review Required in correspondence and indicate “undeterminable edoc: #” in their EE Eligibility note

- When CICIC website indicated the institution is recognized, the notes will indicate Canadian institution is “verified"
- When CICIC website indicates the institution is authorized, notes will include statement “education institution authorized for certain programs; review required”.
- When CICIC website indicates the institution is registered, notes will include statement “unable to verify Canadian institution; review required”. When you search an institution on the CICIC website and the legal status is blank, we will treat them as a registered status and your note will include the statement “unable to verify Canadian institution; review required”.
EQUIVALENCY THAT IS NOT CANADIAN

- If the client provided an ECA report that shows an equivalency for any country other than a Canadian equivalency this is considered to be not acceptable and the file is to be rejected.

There are various ways to potentially locate an old passport number if the client didn't provide it. Some current passports have references to the previous passport number.

Examples would be: India-the last page refers to the previous passport number. If it is blank, this indicates there was no previous passport; China – the previous number is usually listed on the second page, if provided by the client; Bangladesh (among other countries) – the previous ppt is listed on the BioData page. Each country is different in what is provided.

## APPENDIX L

## VERIFYING AND ATTACHING MEDICALS TO UCI

## VERIFYING MEDICALS

The client's upfront medical is uploaded in the client's Incoming Correspondence. All medicals should be opened, verified, and the UMI/IME/UF number noted prior to attaching to a UCl. Once this is complete, along with the proper search and promotion procedures (see Appendix K), follow the instructions below to properly attach the medical to the UCl.

Each client provides an upfront Medical, or a Medical Information sheet. The client's upfront medical information is often located within the client's T-number, or within a client's existing UCI. If the medical information is found within an existing UCI this means the medical has already been attached to that UCl, verify that the medical information is correct prior to returning to the application.

## APPENDIX M

## RCMP SCREENING/CLEARANCE

If the client has additional names not included on the application they must be entered in the Client's Name tab within the UCI, prior to starting RCMP Screening.

ONLY Initiate RCMP screening for any spouse/dependents on file 18 years of age and older who have been in Canada for 6 months or more who does not require Biometrics (i.e. non-accompanying) and has not provided an RCMP Police Certificate up front.

RCMP Screenings are to be initiated for NON ACCOMPANYING Spouse/CL and Dep 18+, between the ages of 18 and 65 who have resided in Canada for at least 6 months or more in a row.

A client may submit an RCMP Criminal Record Check with their application. Acceptable RCMP Criminal Record Checks contain the client's fingerprints and their photo (see example below). RCMP Criminal Record Checks must be issued within 6 months prior to the application Rec'd Date in order to be noted. Any Canada RCMP Criminal Record Checks issued prior to that date are not noted.

Example of a valid RCMP Criminal Record Check:

of the RCMP check

If an RCMP Screening must be initiated in GCMS, complete the following steps:

- On the PR Screen, Admissibilities tab, Criminality subtab
- If no client(s) are listed, complete the following steps; Click New Click the ellipsis under the UCI column, the Client Pick Applet window will appear Click on the client you wish to create a criminality line for and click OK then
proceed

Click the dropdown arrow under Type field. Select RCMP Screening Click the dropdown arrow under Status field. Change from Not Started to Submitted Repeat these steps for each person on the file that requires an RCMP Screening Press CTRL+S Status must show either “Fingerprints Required” or “No Reportable Trace” prior to moving forward (click Alt+Enter to refresh)

If the person you are running a RCMP Screening for has a name that is more than 24 characters in length you will need to request RCMP Fingerprints for that client. Change the status from Retransmission Required to Submitted to Fingerprints Requested. Send a request for RCMP Fingerprints (see Appendix U).

If RCMP status generates to Fingerprints Required, RCMP Fingerprints will have to be requested (seeAppendix U). RCMP status MuST be changed to "Fingerprints Requested" after request letter is sent.

## either Former or AKA as the Name Type

As Program Assistants, we are only adding AKA names under the following situations:

If there is an alternate name provided on the following documents:

Legal Change of Name Document Marriage Certificate Birth Certificate If we receive a letter from the client and it specifically says they go by another name (affidavit, or client information) If the ECA clearly indicates that different name/names were provided (we will not go looking for it otherwise on the individual education documents)

If an alternate name was provided but not listed on the above mentioned documents it does not have to be entered as an AKA in GCMS after promotion at this time. (ex: on diplomas) PASSPORTS

In the instances where you have a passport with an unusual naming situation you should still be accessing the Naming Policy website for guidance on how to enter alternate names from the passport as AKAs as found in the R10 manual (Appendix O and Appendix N). This covers items like parents names on passports in the VIZ section, truncated names, patronymics and matronymics,
accented letters, and punctuation, etc....

# Visa-required countries

Travellers from the following countries need a visa to come to Canada by any method of travel — plane, car, bus, train or cruise ship.

Note: Holders of an alien's passport and stateless individuals need a visa to

travel to or transit through Canada. Following a visa imposition, individuals from the now visa-required country who held a valid electronic travel authorization (eTA) need a visa to travel to or transit through Canada. Their eTA is inactive as of the date of the imposition. A 6 C D F G H J K M N 0 E L P U V w Y Q R S T x 2 Show all

### ADDING FEES FROM PROVIDED RECEIPTS

Occasionally the client will provide additional fees with their incoming correspondence such as Biometrics Fees or the Right of Permanent Residence Fee (RPRF).

## To add the fees to the application complete the following steps:

#### To allocate fees:

Go to fees tab

### Under payments click NEW

Add receipt number under the IPRMS Receipt # Hit Tab and the rest of the information will populate, click alt+enter to refresh and a payment number will appear Scroll down to the Fees section and ensure there is a line available to allocate the fees to. If there is a line for the fees to allocate to, scroll up to the payment section and click Allocate. If there is no line under the fees section, create one by clicking new, and selecting the applicable fee line Once the fee line is generated, scroll up to the Payment section and click Allocate.

## The fees will automatically allocate to the payment line that was outstanding.

### Re-review fees to ensure they're accurate before proceeding

#### REQUEST LETTER

as incomplete.

Once the language test and/or Schedule A for gaps, etc. has been received move the file forward.

SIGNATURE Authority to disclose personal information By submitting this form, you consent to the release to Canadian government authorities of all records and information any go vent authority, including police, judicial and state authortslcoutrisinwhcouavdmayssnyourbonmingastition,astschagests,cotionansnnc.Tsimatonweuse ttymmuan Declaration of applicant understand that misrep Canada. note does not indicate that the client has a rep or designated individual.

## EDITING AND SENDING A REQUEST LETTER

Note: Letter editing will vary depending on the situation and/or type of letter being edited.

If required see your RA for letter inserts not found in the manual

- Verify client's information on the letter; Name, UCI, item being requested, etc. o Locate the correct blurb in the Request Letter Templates section of the manual
and copy it. Highlight the blurb that pulled in from your item line and paste the correct blurb over it. Letters are not to be freehanded. There are specific approved blurbs to use found in this manual. 0Ensure you leave the clients name and UCI when inserting the letter template. 0Leave the bullets so your letter is clean and professional looking.

- Add the due date (date entered in GCMS prior to generating the letter) to the line "Please upload these documents to your IRCC secure accountby: DATE Make sure "Please upload these documents to your IRCC secure account by: DATE" is highlighted, centered, bolded and size 12 font or 14 font depending on the letter (if your signature only is pushed to a second page, edit the letter so it is all on one page). These editing settings are located with the Home tab of the word document.
0 日 电 DRev References Insert File Home Mailings View Add-ins 一X Cut A 会↓ T 12 Aa 三三 Times New Ro 三·三 Copy BU A Paste ac X2 x2 三- Format Pain 7 Clipbo Font Paragraph

- Change the line indicating the number of days the documents must be received by to either reflect 7 days, 30 days, or 60 days depending on the document being requested
Medicals – 30 days ECA (Only applicable when Designated Org. contacts client to authorize IRCC review) – 30 Days Police Certificates – 60 days All Other Items - 7days

## Example of Letter After Editing

Immigration, Refugees Immigration, Réfugiés and Čitizenship Canada et Citoyenneté Canada

Date: August 11, 2022

# UCI: Application:

Email:

### Dear

This is in reference to your application for permanent residence in Canada. In order for us to continue the review of your application, we require the following documents: 7 ITEMS THAT WERE REQUESTED WILL BE LISTED HERE

## Please upload and submit these documents to your IRCC secure account by

### August 18, 2022.

Please note:

#### The above information/documents must be receivedwithin 7 days from the date of this

correspondence. If this deadline is not met, your application will be assessed based on the information that was provided with your application and your application could be rejected for being incomplete.

FAILURE TO RESPOND TO THIS REQUEST MAY RESULT IN THE REJECTION OF YOUR APPLICATION, NO OTHER REMINDER WILL BE SENT TO YOU If the documents are not already in English or French, all documents must be accompanied by

#### English or French translations by certified translators. When original documents are requested,

you must upload the original documents to your IRCC secure account. We do not accept copies signed by notaries. For more information on permanent residence in Canada, please visit our web site at: [http://www.canada.ca/immigration](http://www.canada.ca/immigration). Thank you for the interest you have shown in Canada.

# Sincerely,

## REQUEST LETTERS FOR PCS

Some countries require an official request letter from CiC in order for the applicant to apply for a PC. Program Assistants will send request letters for these PCs if the

## application is otherwise R10 complete

- Both the General Police Certificate Request letter and the Request for documents letter may be used to request PCs. It is important to read ALL the information on the How To Website, this will determine which letter to send
## o Some countries will release the PC directly to the client. For these countries the

Request for documents letter is sent (Template 2) o Some countries only release the PC directly to IRCC. For these countries the General Police Certificate Request letter (Template 17), that includes CIO's

## mailing and courier addresses, must be sent

- Complete request letter steps 1-6. For step 7, create a Police Certificate item line if required. In the comments field, indicate the specific country (ex: Please provide a police certificate for Hong Kong) Complete remaining request letter steps and move the file forward

## PC REQUEST LETTER GUIDE

Police Certificate Request when Best Efforts Met – (60 days):

Use Template 2: Request for documents. Item line: Police Certificate Request letter blurb:

Police Certificate: You have provided proof that you applied for your(insert country

name)police certificate. To avoid further delays in processing of your application, please upload the (name of certificate from How To)to your IRCC secure account upon receipt.

RCMP (60 days) – Fingerprints Required: (for non-accompanying Spouse or 18+Dep that spent 6 months or more in Canada; RCMP screening results indicate Fingerprints Required, change to Fingerprints Requested and request)

Use Template 2: Request for documents. Item line: RCMP Criminal Record Check Request letter blurb:

RCMP Criminal Record Check: Please provide an RCMP Certified Criminal Record Check based on fingerprints. Please check the CIC website (How to obtain a police certificate-Canada) and refer to the criminal record check link. Complete all steps as instructed on the RCMP website. You will find the link below.

[http://www.rcmp-grc.gc.ca/cr-cj/fing-empr2-eng.htm](http://www.rcmp-grc.gc.ca/cr-cj/fing-empr2-eng.htm)

If requesting FBI Clearances or fingerprints for RCMP checks, use “FBI Clearance” and “RCMP Criminal Record Check” as the item lines.

Reminder: Use the FBI and RCMP letter inserts in the corresponding request letter (for RCMP See Editing a RCMP Letter within this appendix)

For FBI requests use the following: You have provided proof that you applied for your USA FBI police certificate. To avoid further delays in processing of your application, please upload the FBI Certificate to your IRCC secure account upon receipt.

Common PC Request Countries and Templates to Use:

Note: these are not the only request countries for PCs – refer to the HowTo for specific country

information: [https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-](https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-) citizenship/services/application/medical-police/police-certificates/how.html

Singapore: Item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 16 *if PC provided & doesn't cover full time, request new one using the provided blurb under Request Letter Templates. Colombia: No item line – Use Template 17. *if client indicates they already applied for Colombia PC – send for TL review (email subject line: Colombia PC) Hong Kong: No item line – Specific HK PC request letter template in letter kit. *add HK as secondary office, steps in Appendix F Fiji: Item line: Police Certificate. If COR is Fiji – Use Template 28. If COR is not Fiji – Use Template 29. Japan: Item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 2. Netherlands: Item line: Police Certificate. – Use Template 2. Mexico: If COR is Mexico: item line: Police Certificate – Use Template 2. If COR not Mexico, no item line – Use Template 17. ** If client indicates that Mexico PC has already been applied for:Refer to the TL so they can check to see if PC was provided and if acceptable/how to note it (email subject line: Mexico PC)

# ***If Mexico PC is provided & current COR is NOT Mexico:

We cannot accept Mexico police certificates provided directly by clients from the embassy or consulate for clients that are not currently residing in Mexico; a new Mexico PC must be requested so it can come directly to IRCC and not to the client. For new Mexico PC requests for those that were provided up front: Use Template 30 - Mexico Police Certificate Request-EN.

For clients whose COR is Mexico: there is no change in the process. Make sure you review the How-To website for the applicable guidelines.

mmigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canadaet Citoyenneté Canada Information disclosed under the Access to Information Act L'information divulguée en vertu de la loi sur l'accès à l’information

There are several Countries on the How To website with the following: “You do not have to provide a police certificate from (Country) unless we ask for one." If the PA, Spouse or DEP 18+ has been to one of these Countries for a total of 6 months or more,we are to send a request letter for the PC and the Recommendation is Review Required.

SOME Countries that will not release PC to clients who are not citizens/residents or have lived there less than a year (always check the how-to for the most up to date information):

- Malaysia: If client there less than 1 year, under criminality recommendation: Indicates residence in Malaysia for less than 1 year
- Saudi Arabia: If client is not a PR of Saudi Arabia, under criminality recommendation: Previous Residence in Saudi Arabia

Qatar: Some Police certificates from Qatar do not cover the entire time residing in Qatar. Qatar does not provide certificates to clients while on visitor visas, only when they receive resident status, which explains the missing time. If a Qatar PC does not cover the entire time spent in that country; we will continue processing, note the PC as “Review Required” and change the EE Eligibility note to “Review Required” for the recommendation.

Benin: If client is not resident, under criminality recommendation: Previous residence in Benin

•Burma (Myanmar): If client does not currently reside there and cannot get a police certificate: Previous residence in Burma (Mayanmar)

##### FRENCH EE APPLICATIONS R10 PROCEDURES

SToP: Before you send a file to the French inbox, keep in mind the inbox is for translations and sending French letters. If you have an RA situation, the file must still go to the regular CIO Express Entry RA inbox first.

##### Complete French EE Application

- If the French application appears to meet R10, enter the GCMS complete note with the addition of the following words entered at the TOP of the note: Recommend R10 Complete The Program Assistant will assign the file toRC-C13
### EXCEPTION: All CEC and FST should be assigned to RC-C04 regardless of COR

and Language as these applications will always be worked in Canada.

- A bilingual employee will review file for R10, review all French correspondence and add applicable GCMS notes and assign file accordingly
When an application appears to meet R10, is ready to move forward, and requires a letter to be sent to the client requesting an item (for example, when the client has lived in a country that requires us to send a request letter for a Police Certificate).

Send an email to the French inbox asking for the request letter to be sent first. Park the file and

### leave it in your GCMS code until you receive the confirmation email back saying correspondence

has been sent. Once the request letter has been sent, then add your note in GCMS and assign the

#### file to the appropriate code.

## APPENDIX X

## REJECTING (CANCELLING) AN APPLICATION

Once an application has been confirmed to not meet R10 and has been promoted, follow the instructions below to request refund of fees, cancel the application, add an organization and entity, and send correspondence to the client.

All applications MUST be promoted prior to being cancelled. The PA's UCI information MUST be updated as per Appendix Q (Existing UCIs) or Appendix N (New UCIs),

# STOP

and Picked-In as per Appendix P. Before promoting, cancel the Biometric-RCMP sub activities under the Criminality tab (see Appendix KK)

- Verify the lock in date is correct
- Cancel any Admissibilities items that are “In Progress”.
If unable to cancel the In Progress items, refer to RA.

- Identify these items by referring to the Assessments column on the PR screen
Assessments Elgibility: Not Started Security: Not Started HIRV: Criminality: In Progress Org Crime: Misrepresentation: Medical; Info Sharing:Complete

- If the item line is In Progress ex: Criminality, refer to RA via email with subject line "CANCEL CRIMINALITY" and in the email include the application number and any other items in progress if required and the reason for rejection. MARK THIS EMAIL AS HIGH IMPORTANCE! DO NOT PROCEED TILL THE RA RESPONDS. Wait for the okay from the RA to proceed with the next steps
- Click Fees tab Verify fee lines are properly allocated (if not, refer to Appendix R) Click Refund Info, a window will appear
- Click ellipsis in the UCI/Party ID field
- Choose the PA's UCI, the PA's address will populate

## HOW TO DISASSOCIATE A CANADIAN/PERMANENT RESIDENT

A dependent (child, spouse/common-law partner) must provide proof they are a Canadian (birth certificate, passport, etc.). If unable to verify refer to RA.

A dependent (child, spouse/common-law partner) that is a Permanent Resident must be verified by the RA. Wait for the RA to advise before proceeding.

## APPENDIX BB

## DEFINITION OF A DEPENDENT CHILD

Effective October 24, 2017, the definition of a “dependent child" is: A dependent child, in respect of a parent, is a child who:

a. has one of the following relationships with the parent:
i. is the biological child of the parent, if the child has not been adopted by a person other than the spouse or common-law partner of the parent ii.is the adopted child of the parent
b. is in one of the following situations of dependency:
i.is less than 22 years of age and is not a spouse or common-law partner ii.is 22 years of age or older, has depended substantially on the financial support of the parent since before the age of 22 and is unable to be financially self-supporting due to a physical or mental condition Former Definitions of a Dependent Child:
Between August 1, 2014, and October 23, 2017, the definition of "dependent child" was as follows: A dependent child, in respect of a parent, is a child who

a. has one of the following relationships with the parent:
i.is the biological child of the parent, if the child has not been adopted by a person other than the spouse or common-law partner of the parent is the adopted child of the parent ii.
b. is in one of the following situations of dependency:
i.is less than 19 years of age and is not a spouse or common-law partner ii. is 19 years of age or older and has depended substantially on the financial support of the parent since before the age of 19 and is unable to be financially self-supporting due to a physical or mental condition For additional information refer to the following link:
[http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/perm/definitions-eng.aspx?letterNum=4](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/perm/definitions-eng.aspx?letterNum=4)

## OVER-AGED DEPENDENTS

The Program Assistant is responsible to note if dependents on an application are over-aged.

If an application with an over-aged dependent(s) is identified: Process application and complete R-10 procedures Leave in your GCMS code Email the RA, use the following Template:

Email Subject line: Over-aged Dependent

# Hello,

Overage dependent Identified

Application Number: Client Name: DOB: Please advise.

# Thank you

The RA will review and determine action, and advise the Program Assistant how to proceed.

## APPENDIX CC

## ADDING A DEPENDENT TO AN APPLICATION

Before adding a Dependent the RA MusT confirm that the dependent in question should be added to the application. The RA will verify what documents and/or fees must be requested and received

There are two ways to receive a request to Add a Dependent/Spouse/Common-Law Partner (DEP/SP/CL).

R10 in Progress-The client submits a request to add a DEP/SP/CL via Client Enquiry. R10 Complete-File is forwarded to a Program Assistant via email from the team leader.

CLIENT REQUESTS TO ADD A CANADIAN DEPENDENT

- If the client submits a request to add a DEP/SP/CLP and the individual is a Canadian Citizen adding the dependent is not required, as long as there is proof of Citizenship provided (Birth Certificate, Passport, PR card). For PR status citizens, refer to the RA, park the file, and await direction prior to proceeding, PR status must be verified.
- Note the following for requests with confirmed Canadian citizens at the bottom of EE note: Dependent not associated to application as [spouse/common-law partner/child] is a [Canadian Citizen OR Permanent Resident of Canada]. Proof of status provided.
ADDING A DEPENDENT- R10 IN PROGRESS

- If the application in progress contains Client Information or a Client Enquiry that requests to add a DEP/SP/CLP, make note of the DEP/SP/CLP to be added, and continue to review the remaining documents for R10 completeness. If the file is R10 incomplete
Proceed with rejection (See Appendix X) If the file is R10 complete Promote as per R10 processing procedures, do not add EE Eligibility note Park the file Refer to the RA for review and await direction. Once confirmation is received, add the DEP/SP/CLP follow the steps under Adding a Dependent R10 Complete When the documentation is received, fees allocated, and file is now R10 complete proceed to add in the EE Eligibility note, include the added DEP/SP/CLP in the note, ensure criminality is noted appropriately (see Appendix F) Enter the following at the bottom of your EE Eligibility note: Spouse/CLP/Dep child added after ITA

HOW TO USE SNAGIT

The Snaglt program captures images of documents to compare and view multiple items Adjust the window the document opens in so all required information is visible Click the spyglass near the start menu and search for Snagit, you will select the Apps Snagit 2023. When this is open, right click on it in the task bar and select Pin to Taskbar to keep the icon on your taskbar at all times. You can also pin to taskbar the Snagit 2023 Editor – where you will view your captured images. When the Snagit Tool is open it looks similar to the image below – image may vary depending on preferences that are set up on your system

Click on the screen containing the document to capture or click and drag a border around the image section required (ex: Passport Bio Data information) A tool bar will appear with the highlighted image, click the Camera Icon to capture o457 X 173 2 X

The Snaglt Editor program will open, displaying the image captured Some of the features included in Snaglt: Tool bar for editing features (ex: Rotating an image: click Image, Rotate, drop down menu, and select direction) oZoom feature OThumbnails of all recently captured documents are displayed (click on the thumbnail to enlarge the image)Delete previously Snagged documents by clicking the Library tab at the top of the Snaglt program highlight the document(s) you wish to delete, right click and select Delete OTo active the quick One Click feature of Snagit, have the tool open, click File > Capture Preferences> toggle Capture Window & OneClick – the mini tool will appear on the top or sides of your screen depending on preferences. # APPENDIX II

RECONSIDERATIONS

For all reconsideration requests, any information received after the decision has been made is not acceptable. Exceptions to this procedure may arise due to circumstances at the time. Each case should be examined in detail on an individual basis.

## Examples of Reconsideration Requests:

Example 1: You rejected the application for a missing FBI clearance. PA submits a reconsideration request advising proof the FBI clearance was applied for was provided with the original submission. When you review the file again, you realize you over looked the FBI clearance application and tracking info of application to FBI office in Clarksburg, West Virginia. This is considered best efforts and the file should have moved forward in processing, not rejected. Since an error was made in processing, you must reopen the file if R10 is passed. NOTE: If something else is missing and R10 is incomplete, file will not be reopened. Discuss with your RA prior to sending updated rejection letter.

Example 2: You rejected the application as the PC on file was issued before the client's last date of residence in that country. PA submits a reconsideration request with a newer version of the police certificate and advises they made a mistake in uploading and forgot to include the new version of the police certificate. Decision remains in this case, no error was made during processing on the department's behalf and PA submitted an application that was R10 incomplete. In some cases, notification of a reconsideration request is received via email from CSU.

CSU Procedures:

Upload the email request to the application Receive the reconsideration request when they run the Provided Report. Notify the Program Assistant by email of the reconsideration request. Forward a template response to the client (Edit template to request applicant monitor their IRCC secure account for further information).

# APPENDIX JJ

## CHARGING FILES IN GCMS

RC-C14: GCMS Issues. Charge the application to this code when there is a GCMS issue. Example: unable to promote an application

1. Take Snagit of error 2. Send an email to your team leader (template Appendix GG), include UCI's 3. Assign to RC-C14 4. The team leader will refer to GCMS manager
- RC-C13: French. Applications containing French Correspondence requiring additional review will be charged to this code. A bilingual Agent will review the correspondence and forward the application accordingly. See Appendix V RC-C15: International Network. For FSW/PNP if current COR is any country other than Canada or the USA, R10 complete application
- RC-C04: Centralized Network ALL CEC/FST files are assigned to this code. For FSW/PNP assignment is based on if current COR is Canada or the USA, R10 complete application
- RC-C01: QA Code. To be used when applications are being reviewed by a mentor for quality assurance before being finalized
Note: If your applications are being QA'd your mentor MAY advise you
 which code to charge your application to if not using the generic code.
- RC – C20: Biometric fees were requested.
- RC – C16: To be used when there are Biometrics issues i.e. incorrect Biometric fees, overpayments
Note: Files only get referred to the RC-Ci6 code once file revlew is completed, EE
 Eligibility note is entered, and the file is moving forward as R10 complete.
Add a separate note in GCMS 'Referred for Biometric Review'

- Your Code: When an application has been parked, awaiting response from the RA, etc., the application must be charged to your GCMS code. Run your dashboards each morning to view what documents have been received
- Please note – depending on processing requirements, additional Assign-To codes may be added if there is a need for a special holding code for projects and program requirements.

AFTER PROMOTION – REJECTIONS

If the Biometric – RCMP line under the Criminality Sub Activities has a Biometric #, email your TL before rejecting and wait for instructions on how to proceed.

If the file is a rejection, Biometrics should have been cancelled prior to promotion; Biometrics – RCMP lines in the Sub Activities under Admissibility – Criminality lines were changed to Cancelled prior to promotion, the BIL letter should NOT have sent.

Complete rejection as normal with addition to the following steps:

- Refund Biometric Fee in addition to the other fees as per the process outlined in Appendix X. If the BIL letter is sent in error for a rejection (ex: the program assistant did not select cancel for the Biometrics – RCMP line in Admissibilities, Criminality), or the file was held for any reason, the BIL letter was sent and becomes a rejection, the program assistant is to send an email to their team leader indicating the BIL letter was sent in error or sent due to holding the file, and provide the application number. Hold the file in your code. Do not refund fees until advised how to proceed. The Biometric fees may not be refundable if the client already had their Biometrics completed

Dear «Given_Name» «Family_Name»,

This is in reference to your application for permanent residence in Canada. On July 31st, 2018, biometrics collection (biographic data, fingerprints and a photograph) became a requirement when you apply for permanent residence. Although you have paid $85 in biometric fees, you are required to pay the S170 Biometric Family Rate. Please pay the outstanding SS5 balance. Please note: On June 14, 2023, the temporary public policy exempting foreign nationals from the biometric requirement was lifted. Any permanent resident applicant and accompanying dependents who are between the ages of 14-79 will now be required to submit biometrics, unless otherwise exempt. For more information please see the link below: Program delivery update: Removal of the public policy exempting foreign nationals with previous biometrics on file who are applying for permanent residence from biometrics-Canada.ca

 Quantity.

# Appendix LL

PASSPORTS AND TRAVEL DOCUMENTS

Clients are required to provide a valid passport or travel document for travel to Canada. A passport or travel document is considered valid if it's:

legal not expired, and accepted by us (Immigration, Refugees and Citizenship Canada) The following documents are not acceptable (by IRCC) as travel documents to Canada as they are considered unreliable and are NOT valid for travel to Canada:

Documents we don't accept (unreliable travel documents) The following documents are considered unreliable and are not valid for travel to Canada:

- Any Passport Issued by Somalia
- Non-machine readable passports issued by the Czech Republic
- Temporary passports issued by the Republic of South Africa
- Provisional passports issued by Venezuela
Please be advised, effective immediately if these unreliable documents are provided as travel documents on an application, the application is a REJECTION, the file is incomplete as the applicant has not provided a valid passport/travel document. They are not accepted by IRCC. The Program Assistant will insert into the REJECTION Letter the following link to the public information page, so the applicant can see the acceptable/unacceptable documents. Public information page below: [https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/passport-travel-document.html](https://www.canada.ca/en/immigration-refugees-citizenship/services/passport-travel-document.html)

# APPLICATION RECEIVEDON OR AFTER OCTOBER 1, 2023

- As of October 1, 2023, upfront medicals (UFM) are NO LONGER REQUIRED to be provided upfront for Express Entry!
- However, if a medical is provided there is a temporary public policy in place from October 6, 2022 to October 6, 2024 which exempts some In-Canada clients from providing a new Medical.
# *PLEASE NOTE*

Every file can have any combination of these scenarios. Please review every file on a client by client basis.

Scenario 1

- Client provides a valid medical (regardless of COR). Attach the medical to the application and move the application forward.
- Update note in the ADMM to the following (situation depending): Primary Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Spouse Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Dependent Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes
Scenario 2

- Client provides an expired medical (Regardless of COR). If the medical is an M1, M2 or M3 and was issued within 5 years of the application received date please attach the medical to the application. Enter all required information on the Medical Reassessment Spreadsheet (formerly called Medical TPP Spreadsheets). Move the application forward. DO NOT REQUEST A NEW MEDICAL. Update note in the ADMM to the following (situation depending): Primary Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Spouse Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Dependent Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Email sent to CIO Sydney Medical for possible medical extension (To be added to the bottom of the Medicals section of R10 Note)

## Scenario 3

- Client does not provide a medical with their application.
3. Please review any T#’s in GCMS to determine if the client has a valid or expired medical. If a medical is present please attach the medical to the UCl, then to the application (if it is an M1, M2 or M3 and was issued within 5 years of the application received date). Enter all required information on the Medical Reassessment Spreadsheet. Move the application forward. DO NOT REQUEST A NEW MEDICAL.
- Update note in the ADMM to the following (situation depending): Primary Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Spouse Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Dependent Applicant Medical Attached to UCI: Yes Email sent to ClO Sydney Medical for possible medical extension (To be added to the bottom of the Medicals section of R10 Note)
4. If no medical can be found on any T#’s in GCMS then please request a new medical for the client.

## Proof of Funds (Incoming Correspondence):

Proof of funds IS a FSW requirement. If Proof of Funds is not provided, an LMIA supported job offer is required. If the client was not prompted for Proof of Funds (no Item line) but there is an Item line for Offer of Employment, the Program Assistant will move the file forward

- If the client was not prompted for Proof of Funds but there is no item line for Offer of Employment, the Program Assistant will request the Proof of Funds and move the file forward.
NOTE: Proof of Funds is not an item line evaluated by the program assistant, at the present time. Until otherwise noted the Program Assistant is to ensure there is an item line for Proof of Funds only.

If the PA has a 0, or A, level NOC and does not meet the language test benchmark; refer to instructions in Appendix E for benchmarks not met.

FST

A Certificate of Qualification from a Canadian province, OR an Offer of Employment from a Canadian Employer accompanied by an LMIA or Work permit is an FST requirement.

NOC SHOULD BE SKILL LEVEL 0, A, OR B NOC can be found under Clients & Parties tab

NOC CODING SYSTEM Skill Level 0 – First digit is a 0. (The second digit is the Skill Type) Skill Level A – When the second digit is a 0 or 1

### Skill Level B – When the second digit is a 2 or 3

EE ELIGIBILITY TAB:

#### Education subtab:

Proof of Education is NOT a FST requirement.

- If applicable, SnagIt all ECA information
- A client may submit either: Canadian Education Credential Foreign Credential (Translation/Original not required) ECA (Educational Credential Assessment)
- All education for the PA and Spouse must be reviewed if provided See Appendix J for more information regarding ECA and Canadian Education.
Canadian Diplomas issued in Latin do not require a translation.

The client may upload: Letter of Offer from a Canadian Employer AND a LMIA OR Work

Proof of Funds (Incoming Correspondence):

Proof of funds IS a FST requirement. If Proof of Funds is not provided, an LMIA supported job offer is required.

- If the client was not prompted for Proof of Funds (no Item line) but there is an Item line for Offer of Employment, the Program Assistant will move the file forward If the client was not prompted for Proof of Funds but there is no item line for Offer of Employment, the Program Assistant will request the Proof of Funds and move the file forward.
NOTE: Proof of Funds is not an item line evaluated by the program assistant, at the present time. Until otherwise noted the Program Assistant is to ensure there is an item line for Proof of Funds only.

#### Language subtab:

Language test is a FST requirement. Acceptable Language tests are IELTS, CELPIP, and TEF.

- To view language test details: Click on EE Eligibility tab, Language subtab Only review Language lines with a status of Received

CLB 5 (English) or NCLC 5 (French) for speaking and listening, and CLB 4 (English) or NCLC 4 (French) for reading and writing.

## NAMING PROCEDURES: ENTERING/UPDATING NAMES IN GCMS

## When entering names in GCMS:

Verify the Family Name, Given Name(s), Gender, DOB, Country of Birth and, Place of Birth. The information must match the client's passport. Verify the name with the MRZ line of the passport. Set the correct name line as Primary or create a new line if changes are required OThe passport MRZ lines should ALWAYS be used when verifying the client's name. Occasionally there is a variation of the name in the VIZ section of the passport, or they have French accents or accent marks included over the letters. In these instances you must consult the Names Policy. oNames Policy: [http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/travel-voyage-eng.aspx](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/travel-voyage-eng.aspx) [http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/gcms-smgc-eng.aspx](http://cicintranet.ci.gc.ca/connexion/tools-outils/id/name-nom/gcms-smgc-eng.aspx) O If the passport does not state the Place of Birth for the client, leave what the client has entered into GCMS. Ex: On a Korean passport it does not show a Place of Birth (city), do not change GCMS to say Korea. Place of Birth should never be a country 0To create a new line for the name: Click New Select Primary under Name Type field Tab across to each field and enter the information as per passport Press CTRL+S keys

## FRENCH ACCENTS OR ACCENT MARKS:

If a client has specified a French accent in his/her name, and it is supported by the VIZ of their passport, the name must be recorded in GCMS as the primary name (these will not show in the MRZ).

If a client has specified a French accent in his/her name, and it is NOT supported by the VIZ of their passport (ex: in a document, or letter, etc), it will be recorded as an AKA in GCMS and the primary name will be recorded as per the MRZ.

The names found in the original language should never be recorded as part of the primary name or printed on citizenship and immigration documents, unless they contain French accents, in which case they are recorded in IRCC's system of record and on IRCC issued documents. Where possible, these names will be recorded as an "AKA" (alias) or "other name" in IRCC's system of record, for verification purposes.

GCMS accepts the following French accents: Â-À - É-Ê - Ë - È - 1 - Î - Ô - Ü - ù - Û - Ç.

To produce the necessary characters in capitals for recording and issuing client's name in GCMS, follow “ALT + CODE" key below: ALT +

### EDITING NOTES IN THE ADMM

The GCMS note will still require editing prior to being added to GCMS. The following are

# examples of some situations where the note must be manipulated to provide the necessary

## information. The details regarding these scenarios is outlined in the R10 manual. Ensure you

## review and understand the R10 manual prior to inputting these notes.

## POLICE CERTIFICATES

REMINDER: All police certificates provided MUST be listed in the EE Eligibility note

Per a COVID 19 process criminality needs to include "pending Biometrics RCMP results" to the criminality recommendation. This has replaced the need for running an RCMP check in GCMS under most circumstances (with the exception of a Dep 18+ or SP that is NON ACCOMPANYING and has spent 6 months or more in a row Canada (see Appendix M for instructions)

- If the PA or SP have spent more than 6 months in Saudi Arabia, but do not currently live there and did not provide a Saudi Arabia PC, enter:Previous Residence in Saudi Arabia, in the GCMS note BELOW the criminality recommendation line. If this is the only item, Criminality recommendation is Pass Criminality. This may also apply to Benin, or other
-----POLICE CERTIFICATE----- Primary Applicant India Police Certificate: Provided-NRT R10 OK Recommendation: Passed criminality – pending Biometric-RCMP Results Previous Residence in Saudi Arabia

### similar situation countries.

- If the client supplies a PC issued more than six months prior to the application received date, and the client has travelled back to this same country (for less than 6 months) after the issued date, move the file forward. Criminality recommendation is Review Required and enter:Travel to after PC obtained, please review, in the GCMS note BELOW the criminality recommendation line
-----POLICE CERTIFICATE----- Primary Applicant India Police Certificate: Provided-NRT Pakistan police certificate: Provided -NRT R10 OK Recommendation: Review Required – pending Biometric-RCMP Results Travel to Pakistan after PC obtained, please review

If the client returned to this same country for 6 months or more, a new PC is required. If an updated PC was not provided, the file should be rejected

### NOTES REVIEW QUESTIONS

Each note requires editing prior to being entered in GCMS. The following indicates what items should be included in your note, ask yourself the following questions when doing the review of your note: •Review Type: Is this filled in as Program Assistant?

- Application #: Make sure this is included and matches the file you will be processing
- Regulation: (LINE OF BUSINESS OF THE FILE)
- Min Score: (# pulls from GCMS EE Eligibility tab)
- Existing UCl or New UCl(s)
- Are there Stat Questions on this file, they should be noted here, and who they are for, PA or SP
- Is there a representative/Designated Individual on this file or included with this file? • Are medicals attached?
- Are the medicals expired? Did you include the correct extra note?
- - OR-Were Medicals requested? (appendix MM) Did you include the correct extra note?
- Are fees verified?
- Language test results must be noted for PA, if provided by the SP they must be noted for the SP
- Education must be noted for PA, for Foreign Credential it must be noted EXACTLY as the ECA. Foreign credential must be noted if it is provided or not.
- Canadian education noted? Is it undeterminable? Did you include the edoc#?
- Is there a SP? If so, their education must be noted as well Police certificates must be noted if they are provided whether they are required or not. Refer to the notes section of this manual for guidance, if there are stat questions on this file, the recommendation must be changed to review required
- Did you add the Pending Biometrics RCMP results note as required? •Is the AGE listed? Was there any documents set for review such as Client Information or a Client enquiry?
- Did you have any additional notes on the side bar of your ADMM to address before finalizing your note. TIP: ALWAYS review your note prior to adding it to the GCMS Notes tab. Ensure all necessary changes are made and any additional details are added as required.
